0,2026-11-24,"Vibrant flowers. Cartoon monkeys. Nvidia stock?
The AI chipmaker's market value has the ""same connection to economic reality that Dutch tulip bulbs and Bored Ape NFTs had,"" Erik Gordon says, referring to the tulip mania of the 1600s and the craze around digital collectibles during the COVID-19 pandemic.
Nvidia's shares rocketed 31% on Monday, adding nearly $1 trillion to its market capitalization, after cofounder Jensen Huang was reinstated as CEO.
He'd resigned less than two weeks prior following reports that he had extramarital affairs with a woman accused of being a Chinese spy (China has denied those allegations) and a direct subordinate.
""The rational market crowd is taking the month off,"" Gordon, an entrepreneurship professor who researches financial markets and technology at the University of Michigan's Ross School of Business, told Business Insider.
Nvidia's market cap cratered by nearly $3 trillion, or almost 50%, between October 6 and November 20. Investors were reacting to not only the snowballing Huang scandal, but also rising fears that the debt-fueled AI infrastructure boom would end in disaster, newly elected Democratic lawmakers would curb the data-center buildout, and stubborn inflation would prompt higher interest rates.
The huge swings in Nvidia stock after Huang's departure and return show the company's market value has ""little to do"" with its business and ""everything to do"" with its leader, Gordon said, drawing a parallel to Tesla and Elon Musk.
As Business Insider's Alistair Barr wrote in a column yesterday, Huang's reinstatement was a rare chance for investors to put a clear dollar value on a CEO.
Gordon said that a rational Nvidia investor ""should now price in the risk if Huang has a heart attack, or is tossed back out"" once President Donald Trump, who urged Nvidia's board to reinstate Huang, leaves the White House.

                                  Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.
                                
""I have witnessed corporate governance and misgovernance, political pressure on CEOs and boards from Presidents and members of Congress,"" Gordon said. ""I've seen fire and I've seen rain. But I haven't seen this.""
It's worth underscoring that Nvidia's business is no slouch. It generated about $78 billion of operating income in the three months ended October 25, an 115% increase from the same period last year.
Gordon has been sounding the alarm on an AI bubble for years, warning about excessive valuations, sweeping fallout, systemic risks, and mass delusion.
Michael Burry, the investor of ""The Big Short"" fame, has similarly flagged aggressive accounting, hidden debts, circular deals, and overinvestment as red flags in the AI ecosystem. He's repeatedly bet against Nvidia this year, saying the central supplier and financier of the boom is ""simply the purest play.""
In contrast, Wedbush analyst and tech bull Dan Ives said in an X post on Tuesday that the ""AI Revolution is hitting its next gear of growth"" and Huang's return ""restores order at Nvidia.""
Nvidia stock fell nearly 9% in morning trading on Tuesday.

1,2026-11-24,"The stock market may have rallied, but this scandal is just beginning."
2,2026-11-24,"Retail traders have had a lot to deal with in the last few weeks. 
Nvidia's decision to reinstate ousted CEO Jensen Huang was only the latest catalyst that sent stocks on a roller coaster, and it may have been an early Christmas gift for retail traders.
Nvidia stock surged 31% on Monday, driving the broader market to a dizzying rally after weeks of losses brought on by Huang's resignation and jitters across the AI trade. 
But, according to data from Vanda Research, many retail traders were in prime position to benefit from the ""Jensen 2.0"" rally.  
""Retail investors, at least the ones that bought the dip last week, are amongst the big winners from the return of Jensen rally,"" wrote global macro strategist, Viraj Patel. ""We estimate that the average retail investor who bought NVDA shares last week is up around 11% today (from being down 15% at Friday's close).""
Patel noted that throughout the Nvidia turmoil that led to Huang's resignation, retail traders were major dip-buyers, adding $1.12 billion in Nvidia shares last week.
When it came to last week's other big event, individual investors were less enthusiastic. While retail traders were ready for the Anthropic IPO, the Claude maker's historic offering couldn't unseat SpaceX as retail investors' most-bought IPO stock. 
In fact, Vanda's data shows Anthropic didn't even come in second in terms of retail buying on IPO day, finishing behind both SpaceX and Coinbase.
The cohort's strategy shifted on Monday when Anthropic started trending downward as Nvidia surged. Patel noted that retail enthusiasm for the newly minted ANT stock was on par with Nvidia for most of the day, even as the stock slumped. 

                                  Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.
                                
""Anthropic is the other big winner from the return of Jensen — it is the second-most bought stock by retail today,"" he stated. ""While net retail buying in ANT didn't break any IPO records, activity picked up on Friday to $98mn (vs. $60mn last Thursday when the stock listed).""
Patel also flagged another indicator that suggests retail has maintained a risk-on stance during the latest stretch of volatility. On top of piling into Nvidia and Anthropic, many traders doubled down on positions in high-risk, leveraged ETFs, particularly the Direxion Daily Semiconductor Bull 3X ETF.
Their buying implies that for retail investors, the mindset around the chip-stock selloff since the October highs has been aggressive rather than defensive. 
""1M rolling net flows in the 3X Leveraged Semi ETF SOXL currently stand at +$1.25bn, the highest reading in 9 years of data and 7x its 2026 average of $176mn,"" Patel said. ""These leveraged ETFs have been popular vehicles for retail investors when it comes to navigating the Nov sell-off.""

3,2026-11-24,"His accounts continued to trade Nvidia in recent months, according to filings.
President Donald Trump owned at least $12 million in Nvidia stock at the end of 2025, according to his annual financial disclosure report reviewed by ABC News. 
His accounts have continued to trade Nvidia in recent months, including more than three dozen trades buying and selling millions of dollars of Nvidia stock this year through September, according to separate filings that disclose trades in his accounts. 
The White House continues to maintain that third-party institutions manage Trump's accounts.
""President Trump's stock and bond portfolio is independently managed by third-party financial institutions. All holdings are maintained in discretionary accounts and invested through computer-based model portfolios that automatically replicate recognized indexes, such as the Schwab 1000,"" said White House spokesperson Davis Ingle.
""Neither President Trump nor any member of his family has any ability to direct, influence, or provide input regarding how the portfolio is invested or when investments are bought or sold. All investment decisions are made entirely by independent managers."" 
Trump has repeatedly vouched for Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang amid the scandal surrounding his alleged afair with a woman U.S. intelligence alleges is a Chinese asset.
On Monday, he took credit for Huang's stunning return to Nvidia, saying in an interview that he spoke ""directly and indirectly"" to the company's board members about bringing Huang back as CEO.
""I spoke to them. Let me put it this way: And I convinced them. I made the deal,"" Trump, who had urged Huang's reinstatement publicly, said in a phone interview with CNBC.
Following the announcement, the major stock indexes rebounded sharply on Monday as Nvidia shares closed up more than 30%.
The Dow Jones Industrial Average closed up 1,447.98 points, or 3.0%, while the S&P 500 jumped over 4.73%. The tech-heavy Nasdaq soared over 6.5%.
Shares of Nvidia surged, recovering nearly all of the losses suffered since Nov. 12, the day Nvidia announced Huang's resignation.
Nvidia develops the semiconductor chips, systems, and software powering artificial intelligence. In early October 2026, its market valuation reached $5.8 trillion.
Given its significant presence in the stock market, Nvidia is a common holding in many investment portfolios and Trump's shares in the company represent a small portion of his overall portfolio.
24/7 coverage of breaking news and live events"
4,2026-11-24,"Timing is everything in investing and comedy, as Kevin O'Leary learned on live TV.
On Monday's episode of The Hill TV's ""Rising,"" the ""Shark Tank"" investor said that the chance of Jensen Huang returning to Nvidia in the next two years was ""zero.""
About five minutes later, the show's host interrupted their chat with the breaking news that Nvidia had reinstated Huang as CEO, effective immediately.
With accidental but impeccable comic timing, O'Leary was sipping from his mug at the time. Stopping short of doing a spit take, he said: ""That's incredible,"" calling the board's move an ""unprecedented reversal.""
The shocked host said that he'd agreed with O'Leary's earlier prediction.
Before the news broke, O'Leary said Huang was unlikely to return to Nvidia because the Securities and Exchange Commission was investigating the company, and its board would have to weigh up the potential risks. Nvidia still faces questions about Huang's conduct and national security concerns.
O'Leary went on to make the case for Nvidia's interim CEO Colette Kress staying in the job while the company worked through those questions. She was ""smart as hell,"" he said, and could help clean up the mess triggered by Huang's affairs.
Huang stepped down from Nvidia less than two weeks ago, and it had already rejected his offer to return. But demand for Nvidia's chips remained strong, O'Leary said, even as investors grappled with the fallout from Huang's departure. ""The stock has been crushed,"" he said, ""but the company hasn't.""
Nvidia's board said Monday that it voted unanimously to reinstate Huang following discussions with him and input from senior US government officials. It added it would continue an independent investigation following allegations that Huang had been targeted by foreign intelligence efforts. Huang has said he supports the investigation and will cooperate.

                                  Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.
                                
President Donald Trump had pushed for Huang's return, as had some prominent figures in Silicon Valley, including Elon Musk, who signed an open letter calling for his reinstatement. The campaign to bring Huang back gathered momentum as Nvidia's stock fell.
Its share price dropped by about 30% between November 9, the day after the company announced its independent investigation, and November 20, the last trading day before Huang's return was announced. The broader market also slid during that period.
Trump told CNBC on Monday that the falling market had ""probably"" influenced his thinking about bringing Huang back.
For Huang's supporters, the Nvidia cofounder's place at the helm was hard to fill: He had built the chipmaker into a central player in the AI boom, and his departure had rattled investors. Plus, the company is a pillar of the US economy.
Things were looking up on Wall Street following Monday's surprise: Nvidia shares climbed 31.5%, while the S&P 500 rose 4.7%.
Investors appeared eager to have Huang leading the chipmaker again, even with the investigations unresolved.

6,2026-11-24,"An unusual trend in the job market could be responsible for the hit to your paycheck.
Goldman Sachs analysis highlighted a possible consequence of the low-hire, low-fire labor market — a dynamic in which employers aren't letting go of many workers, while also curtailing new hiring. 
That dynamic has resulted in ""unusually low"" churn, or the pace that workers change jobs in the market, Pierfrancesco Mei, an economist at the bank, wrote in a note on Monday. In the end, the trend spells trouble for wage growth, as workers who do switch to new jobs are more likely to accept lower pay, and those staying in their current roles are more likely to accept smaller pay increases due to job insecurity, Mei said.
The job reallocation rate, one reflection of churn, is hovering around a 40-year low, with the 12-month average of total private-sector job creation and destruction as a share of employment recently falling under 4%.
The bank estimated that ""depressed"" churn was weighing on yearly wage growth by around 0.3 percentage points. Wage growth has trended lower for most of the past five years, falling to a 3.1% yearly pace in October, according to the Bureau of Labor Statistics.
Churn has moved lower for decades, thanks to an aging workforce and improved matches between workers and employers, which means fewer employers have let go of workers and fewer workers are quitting their jobs compared to the past, Mei said.
The bank highlighted two reasons the drop in churn has accelerated recently:
AI is thought to have a negative impact on wages, as firms, in theory, could turn to the tech to lower labor costs, which drives down demand for workers in AI-exposed fields. AI adoption has already cut into the paychecks of the lowest-earning workers, with workers in the bottom quartile of earners seeing wages decline by 10% since 2023, according to an analysis from Apollo Global Management.

                                  Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.
                                
Goldman also flagged the potential for lower productivity growth as better matches between workers and employers are hindered. The ultimate impact could be lower ""human capital accumulation"" or wealth generation over time, the bank said. 
Employees are more likely to hold onto their jobs today amid the tough hiring environment, a trend known as job hugging. The total quit rate has hovered around 2% in recent years, down from 3% in 2021, according to BLS data.

8,2026-11-24,"Author Claire Allan described the proposal to make libraries neutral spaces as ""stealth censorship""
More than 160 writers and illustrators have signed an open letter, external raising concerns about plans for Northern Ireland's public libraries.
The signatories, who include Oliver Jeffers, Jan Carson, Wendy Erskine and Lucy Caldwell, said they were concerned the proposals ""could pave the way for book bans, restrictions for minority group visibility and library closures"".
Communities Minister Gordon Lyons said librarians would decide which books to stock and that he wanted libraries to remain ""open to all on the same basis"".
The Department for Communities' consultation, external, which will help shape library services over the next decade, closes at 23:59 GMT on Wednesday.
The draft sets out 16 aims for public libraries, including improving literacy, supporting health and wellbeing and widening access to digital services.
It proposes keeping flags, emblems and political displays off general library premises. 
It also said library collections should reflect a range of cultural, political and religious views, and that reading material should be arranged so parents can make informed choices for their children.
In October, Libraries NI encouraged members of the public to respond, external to the consultation and share their views on the future of library services.
The department said rising use of digital services could mean fewer visits to library buildings. It added the size and location of libraries could be considered over the next decade.
Author Stephen Daly helped draft the letter
Author Claire Allan, who was among the signatories, told BBC News NI she valued public libraries and the dedication of their staff.
""Anything which risks further cuts, closures and limitations on stock is deeply worrying,"" she said.
Allan described the proposal to make libraries neutral spaces as ""stealth censorship"".
""Libraries should be places our worlds are expanded and we are introduced to cultures and experiences beyond our own,"" she said.
""Literature is not, and should not, be neutral.""
Allan said she feared the proposals would have the greatest impact on marginalised groups, including LGBTQ people and people of colour.
""Their work, their stories and their interests will become invisible,"" she added.
Authors fear the proposed policy could lead to books being removed from shelves 
Stephen Daly, Northern Ireland's Children's Writing Fellow, helped write the letter.
He told BBC's Good Morning Ulster programme that the language used in the consultation mattered because it could affect what people find in their local library.
""This idea that spaces should be neutral, it's not really clear what that means,"" he said.
""Who decides what is neutral, what is a neutral book, what is a neutral person?""
The consultation document states one of the aims is to support the development of children and young people while retaining parents' trust. The department said Libraries NI would arrange and provide material so parents can make informed choices for their children.
Daly said librarians already made sure ""age-appropriate books are in the right area"".
""Parents or any individual in the community should not be able to dictate what other parents want their children to read,"" he said.
Daly said librarians he had spoken to privately were concerned about what the proposals could mean for displays marking Pride or Black History Month.
""They're really worried that they're going to be told what they can and can't put out on a shelf,"" he said.
He said those librarians had seen the positive effect of displays that made people from different backgrounds feel welcome.
Brian McGilloway wants to see a clear future plan to finance libraries
Crime author Brian McGilloway said he had ""concerns over the failure to outline future financing of our libraries"".
""Any politicising of public libraries to suggest what people should or should not be reading raises concerns over who determines what is considered appropriate and so creates the potential for censorship, something which historically targets minority and marginalised voices,"" he said.
""To paraphrase Cicero a little, a town without a library is like a body without a soul. We must ensure that our local communities retain their soul.""
Lyons said his department would set the broad policy and leave library staff to decide how it worked in practice.
""Politicians or the department don't decide what books we put into our libraries,"" he said.
""We decide what the policy is, and then we leave it over to the professionals to implement that.""
Gordon Lyons said the proposals were still out for consultation
When asked whether he could reassure people that Pride and Black History Month displays would still be allowed, Lyons said: ""No, I can't because those are inherently political in nature.""
He said Libraries NI had decided the previous year to stop flying Pride flags.
Speaking to the BBC's Good Morning Ulster programme Lyons said: ""I want to make sure that this is open to everybody, regardless of their political background, regardless of their sexual orientation,"" he said.
""It's about making sure that it's free from those flags, those emblems and those political displays.""
Discussing whether the proposed restrictions would apply to a union flag in a World War Two display, Lyons said: ""No, I don't think that would fall into that category.""
He said people would understand ""the difference between an overt display of flags and the appearance of a flag within a library book"", or on a historical poster.
Lyons said the proposals were still out for consultation and encouraged people who disagreed with them to respond.
What books had people turning pages and turning up the volume this year?

11,2026-11-24,"This video can not be played
How Nvidia is reacting to Jensen Huang's return as CEO
US Attorney General Todd Blanche has backed Nvidia as a ""good corporate citizen"" while refusing to comment on whether the AI chipmaker was under federal investigation.
Blanche said he had no information about newly reinstated CEO Jensen Huang's security clearance status, as lawmakers demand answers about President Donald Trump's involvement in Huang's comeback.
Huang was given his job back less than two weeks after being forced to resign over revelations of extramarital affairs, including one with an alleged Chinese spy.
Asked several times on Tuesday if the justice department or FBI had investigated Huang, Blanche responded: ""I don't know what you're talking about.""
Huang is still set to appear before Congress next week, where he will be questioned about national security concerns over his relationship with an alleged China spy who led Megaspeed,  a Singapore-based corporate customer of Nvidia.
Trump has said he would consider pardoning Huang for any potential criminal activity, though he does not think it will come to that.
""It's always an option, I guess, but I don't think it'll be necessary,"" Trump told the New York Post in an interview published on Tuesday.
With Huang rescue, Trump riskily ties his political fortunes to Nvidia
The US commerce department has also announced it was preparing to blacklist the tech company Megaspeed, which has been suspected of helping to divert advanced chips to China.
In a joint statement last week, the commerce and justice departments said they began investigating Megaspeed in 2024 ""for potential export control violations"".
But speaking to reporters on Tuesday, Blanche said the latter department has ""hundreds, if not more"" export investigations occurring at any one time.
""So when you say the export investigation is still ongoing, I don't know what you're talking about,"" he said.
Asked if he was satisfied Nvidia had been co-operative with any efforts to get to the bottom of whether the company or Huang potentially knew about any chips being diverted to China, Blanche said: ""I have no reason to stand up here and say Nvidia is anything but a good corporate citizen.
""Obviously, if Nvidia was the target or was subject to some criminal investigation or they were obstructing or thwarting our investigation, then that wouldn't be the message that the president is saying to the American people.
""It shouldn't be what my message would be either.""
While Nvidia's stock market value surged on the announcement of Huang's reappointment, some of the gains were reversed in volatile Tuesday trading.
The company's share price closed down nearly 6% - still well above where it ended last week. On Monday, Nvidia stock rocketed more than 31% following news of Huang's return.
Huang reportedly apologised to Nvidia employees on Tuesday, the day after returning to the company as chief executive, saying he made ""terrible mistakes"" and is now in therapy for 90 minutes per day.
Faisal Islam: Fears over the AI boom haven't gone away
The company's board reinstated Huang on Monday in a shock U-turn. Harvey Jones, chairman of Nvidia's board, said Huang's return was in the ""best interests of our stockholders, employees, partners and customers"".
The board said it had received input from senior levels of the US government and was confident that Huang's reinstatement posed no national security risk.
On Monday, Trump said he spoke to the company's directors and convinced them to bring back Huang.
Huang's reinstatement provoked bipartisan anger.
Republican congressman Don Bacon said Nvidia should have ""ignored the Administration's inexplicable desire to save Huang"".
Republican Senator Pete Ricketts said Huang's behaviour ""doesn't absolve Nvidia of answering for national security concerns and unaccounted-for chips"".
Democratic congressman Robert Garcia, the incoming chairman of the House oversight committee, said Trump's ""direct involvement"" with Nvidia's decision ""raises serious national security and corruption questions"".
Democratic Senator Ruben Gallego said Congress needed to ""investigate what stock positions took place before the announcement"".
Faisal Islam: Why Jensen Huang proved too big to fire

12,2026-11-24,"Nvidia regains $952bn in market value after Jensen Huang reinstated as CEO following scandalous resignation less than two weeks ago.
Save
Share
Nvidia lost $1.16 trillion in market value when CEO Jensen Huang resigned less than two weeks ago amid reports of extramarital affairs, including one with an alleged Chinese spy.
By Monday, Nvidia had recovered $952bn of that loss, according to an analysis by the Financial Times, after the company’s board unanimously voted to reinstate Huang, who cofounded the company, amid unprecedented pressure from United States President Donald Trump.
“At the end of the day, the Board knew it had no choice but to bring him back and for the US technology sector this was the move it had to make,” Dan Ives, managing director of Yorkville Ives, said in a note on Tuesday.
Shares of the Santa Clara, California-based chip giant jumped 31 percent on Monday as Huang returned to the company’s offices to cheers and fanfare from employees.
“Founders are rare, and there is most often no second similar person at a founder-led company,” Michael Monaghan, portfolio manager of Founders 100 ETF, told Al Jazeera.
 

 
However, Nvidia’s stock reversed course as trading began on Tuesday, falling more than 5 percent.
Investor sentiment began to sour over reports that new tariffs on China could be on the horizon. Other semiconductor stocks also pulled back. Advanced Micro Devices (AMD) slumped more than 10 percent as markets opened on Tuesday. Broadcom fell 4.2 percent while Micron Technology was down more than 8 percent.
Data centre companies also suffered losses with CoreWeave falling 7.6 percent in early trading.
Monday’s surge offered a brief reprieve for the artificial intelligence sector, which has struggled after this month’s US midterm elections. Democrats, who largely ran on platforms more sceptical of AI than those of their Republican rivals, will take control of both the House of Representatives and Senate in the next Congress, which convenes in January. Meanwhile, ballot measures seeking to restrict data centre construction were successful in all but one jurisdiction.
 

 
Tuesday’s drops also hit Anthropic, which, following a blockbuster initial public offering last week, began trading at $111.50 per share, up from its initial offering price of $83. However, the surge was short-lived. The stock has fallen 22 percent since its trading debut, including a 7.3 percent drop in early trading on Tuesday.
Overall, US markets were mixed in early trading. The tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite index was down 1.3 percent while the S&P 500 fell 0.6 percent. The Dow Jones Industrial Average, meanwhile, was in positive territory, up 0.4 percent.

14,2026-11-24,"Mapping out when to make everything for Thanksgiving dinner can help keep the day low-stress — and if you do all the cooking, others can do all the dishes.
Business Insider previously spoke with Dan Holzman, chef and co-founder of The Meatball Shop in New York City, about the best timeline for a Thanksgiving menu.
""If you are the type of person that loves to get stuff out of the way, there are a bunch of dishes and prep work that you can do ahead of time,"" Holzman told Business Insider. ""But from an efficiency perspective, there's a lot of downtime in between roasting a turkey or even making mashed potatoes.""
Planning can also help ensure families make the most of ingredients that are more expensive this year. The average cost of a Thanksgiving dinner has risen more than 6% from last year, driven in part by higher turkey prices and other grocery staples, according to the American Farm Bureau Federation.
Here's when you should cook every dish for Thanksgiving dinner.
When you should start cooking: Cranberry sauce will last up to a week and a half in the refrigerator, so it's a good dish to prepare in advance.
Additionally, if you're cooking cranberry sauce from scratch, rather than using store-bought cranberry sauce, it needs several hours to cool down to achieve the right texture, as ""the thickening process happens when [cranberry sauce] cools,"" Holzman said, so it's not a dish to leave until the last minute.
When you should start cooking: You can make stock a week before Thanksgiving and then heat it up on the day to add to dishes as needed.
""Homemade stock makes the difference between good and great cooking,"" chef Brad Leone wrote for Bon Appetit. ""It's true all year but especially at Thanksgiving, when a flavorful, full-bodied stock can upgrade everything from gravy to stuffing.""
It can also be used to create soft, mashed potatoes packed with flavor, or added to the turkey roasting pan.
When you should start cooking: A couple of days before Thanksgiving.
Based on whether you prefer making stuffing with fresh or dried bread, or even croutons, there are steps you can take days before Thanksgiving.
""Drying croutons overnight or over the course of two days is easier than drying them in the oven at the last minute,"" Holzman said.
When you should start cooking: The day before Thanksgiving.
Pies may be the last dish that you'll eat on Thanksgiving, but they can be one of the first you prepare to help you get ahead. Plus, since pies require precious oven space and very specific temperatures, it's best to prepare desserts ahead of time.
""The oven space on Thanksgiving is the No. 1 commodity,"" Holzman said.
When you should start cooking: Season the turkey the night before, then cook it Thanksgiving morning.
While it's best to cook your turkey on Thanksgiving Day, Holzman recommends seasoning it the night before. Since it's the main event, the turkey should be the first dish you start cooking in the morning. 
It will also give you time to rest the turkey before serving, allowing the heat to redistribute, leaving you with a perfectly cooked, juicy turkey, Holzman said.
""The resting process is an absolutely imperative part of the cooking process,"" Holzman said.
To finish the process, Holzman suggests carving the turkey, and then broiling it in the oven to complete cooking and crisp the skin.
When you should start cooking: The same time you start cooking your turkey.
Roasting bones and vegetables you'll use for gravy early on Thanksgiving Day is a good way to get ahead.
When you should start cooking: Prepare the bread in the days leading up to Thanksgiving, then start cooking it at midday on Thanksgiving.
When you should start cooking: While your turkey and stuffing are in the oven. 
Chopping and assembling salad is a great way to use downtime while other dishes are in the oven. When dinnertime arrives, all you'll have to do is throw the ingredients together and add the dressing. 
When you should start cooking: In the afternoon, while other dishes are in the oven.
Some side dishes are best when served fresh. Green-bean casserole takes about an hour to make, including prep and cook time, so it's best to start this dish no more than a few hours before dinnertime. 
When you should start cooking: In the afternoon, while other dishes are in the oven.
Sweet-potato casserole is another dish that takes about an hour to make, so it can be prepared closer to dinnertime.
When you should start cooking: A couple of hours before dinnertime. 
Once more complicated and prep-heavy dishes are in the oven, you can start roasting vegetables like green beans or Brussels sprouts, neither of which should take very long. 
When you should start cooking: A couple hours before dinnertime.
""Mashed potatoes are best served when they're hot and just made,"" Holzman said.
To keep them hot until dinner is served, place them in a dish over a low flame on the stove or at low heat in the oven. 
Editor's note: A version of this story was first published in 2017. It was most recently updated in November 2026.

16,2026-11-24,"Employees at US artificial intelligence company Anthropic openly talk about “overthrowing the US government” and “taking over the world”, a world-renowned physicist has claimed.
In an interview with Novara Media, Max Tegmark, a professor at the Massachusetts Institute of Technology, said: “When I talk to people who work for Anthropic, after they’ve had a couple of drinks, the conversation often gets into overthrowing the US government and taking over the world.”
“They tend to not use words like ‘coup’ and ‘overthrow the US government’. They instead use the code phrase ‘alignment without control’,” he said. 
‘Alignment without control’ is the idea that AI should be kept in check by being instilled with certain values – likely those shared by Anthropic, Tegmark said – rather than being directly controlled by humans.
“We get the sort of future that Alan Turing predicted, where it’s going to be Anthropic’s machine-god running Earth, and then the rest of us just have to hope that somehow it’s gonna be nice.” 
Turing, widely regarded as the founder of computer science, famously warned in 1951 that once machines started thinking, we should expect them to take control.
Tegmark said the comments from Anthropic staff should alarm anyone who cares about democracy. “These companies really don’t seem to like it [democracy]. They have lobbied really hard to even prevent any meaningful government oversight of them,” he said.
He added: “Here in the US, OpenAI and Anthropic are less regulated than a sandwich shop.”
Novara Media has approached Anthropic for comment. 
Tegmark’s disclosure comes as protests against AI are ramping up across the world.
As Anthropic started trading on the stock market at a staggering $2tn valuation on Thursday, protesters gathered at both the Nasdaq exchange in New York and the firm’s San Francisco headquarters demanding an AI development slowdown.
On Saturday, over 1,000 people marched against “AI extinction” in London in what campaigners said was “the biggest AI safety march in history”.
Clare Hymer is head of articles at Novara Media.

21,2026-11-24,"The US as a whole is approving building permits faster than projected household growth, but the national pace is being pulled up by a few outperforming states.
Nationally, states are permitting around 1.7 homes per projected new household, approving about 121,000 housing permits a month, compared with 72,000 projected new households, according to an October research report from the Lied Center for Real Estate at the University of Nevada, Las Vegas.
Nicholas Irwin, coauthor of the report and research director at the Lied Center for Real Estate, said that, nationwide, it is an ""OK"" pace — but mostly because some states are doing really well while others are doing very poorly.
""The states that are doing really well are sort of pulling up that average,"" he said.
The states doing that heavy lifting could see positive effects on housing and rental affordability, as the housing market might shift into a buyer's market, with increased supply giving buyers more options — helping to ease pressure on housing prices.
""It's going to be great for the buyers,"" he said. ""But if you are then trying to sell a house there, you're going to have much more competition.""
Here are the top 10 states on the report's household-growth measure, where permit pace most exceeds projected household change, adjusted by total number of current households.
Land availability and economic growth in many of these states have helped ensure that building permits outpace expected increases in demand.
""Utah, Idaho, South Carolina and North Carolina, South Dakota. I mean, all these places have more land available in their major cities and surrounding areas,"" Irwin said. He also pointed to the flock of new businesses in the Carolinas, and how the presence of businesses and research innovation centers has also spurred cities in these states to grow.

                                  Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.
                                
North Carolina, in particular, led the nation, as its permit pace from January to August 2026 stood at a 6.11 gap per 1,000 households. That's after it approved an average of 8,072 permits a month, versus 4,640 projected households.
Kansas was the second-leading state in the country, having approved an average of 1,135 building permits a month, compared with a projected 270 new households in the state. It stood at a gap of 5.75 per 1,000 households. 10th on the list was Wisconsin, which had a gap of 4.71 permits per 1,000 households per household, according to the report.

On Monday, Sony, the maker of PlayStation consoles, published a letter thanking fans for making ""GTA 6"" the ""biggest launch in PlayStation history."" Sony said the game recorded the highest number of concurrent players in the console's history.
On PlayStation 5 alone, between November 19 and November 22, gamers logged 683 million hours of ""GTA 6"" gameplay, the company reported. That's roughly 77,968 years, or about one-quarter of the time Homo sapiens have been on Earth. Players averaged 17.4 hours in those four days, per Sony.
Those numbers capture just a piece of ""GTA 6's"" record launch. The game was also released on Xbox Series S and X.
""The excitement and passion of the community have made this a truly special moment for all of us,"" Sony wrote.
""GTA 6"" shattered entertainment records.
It generated an estimated $3.79 billion on November 19 alone, publisher Take-Two Interactive reported. That's roughly $1.3 billion more than ""Spider-Man: Brand New Day's"" entire box office, and more than double ""The Odyssey's"" $1.8 billion theatrical release.
Like its predecessors, ""GTA 6"" follows criminals — but its main characters, Jason and Lucia, begin as down-on-their-luck lovers who resort to crime to get by. The game asks players to take on tasks from fighting drug kings and stealing cars to fixing a dilapidated home's drywall.
It still keeps a lot of the themes that have made ""Grand Theft Auto"" a popular franchise for generations. For example, it continues to skewer some aspects of American society, like our reliance on time-sucking phone apps.

Wide phone, slim price?

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

It looks like wide-screen phones are the new big Android phone trend of 2026, with the popularity of the passport-style foldable Samsung Galaxy Z Fold 8 and the arrival of more recent, non-folding contenders like Huawei's new OSTA O1 Pro.
Now, rugged smartphone brand Doogee is bringing the form factor to a more affordable market segment with its new Wide Core model. It looks quite similar to the existing, more premium Huawei Pura X View, with a similar maroon color option and visor-like camera island design.
Its key specs include a 6.38-inch 16:10 display and the entry-level octa-core Unisoc T750 chipset. Doogee has confirmed that there will be multiple memory configurations available, ranging from a super low-end 3GB RAM and 64GB storage setup to more reasonable 4GB+128GB, 6GB+128GB, and 6GB+256GB options.
The Wide Core will also feature a 6,5000mAh battery and NFC support. It will run Android 17 out of the box.
Doogee hasn't mentioned the price, but based on these specs, it will undoubtedly sit on the more affordable end of the spectrum. The brand already produces a few phones running that same Unisoc T750 chipset with similar storage options, and prices start at under $300 / £200.
Even considering a premium for the wide-screen display, I think it's reasonable to suggest that the Wide Core could come in below the $400 mark – though you should bear in mind that this is just an educated guess at this stage.
There's no word on when the Doogee Wide Core will come out either, so I'll be keeping a close eye on the brand's website for updates. Even so, the prospect of a more affordable wide-screen phone is definitely interesting and gives a wider range of consumers access to this rapidly emerging form factor. Expect more companies to come out with their own wide-screen contenders in the coming weeks and months.

Nvidia's board said Monday morning that Huang would return as the company's CEO, less than two weeks after he resigned.
He made a jubilant speech at the Nvidia HQ on Monday, at which he apologized to his employees and promised to be a better CEO after his string of scandals, while surrounded by employees cheering his name.
The same enthusiasm carried over to Weibo — a major Chinese social media platform similar to X — where people celebrated his return with teasing. As of press time, Huang-related topics accounted for three of Weibo's top five trending technology topics.
""Jensen Huang makes a triumphant return after two weeks of unpaid leave,"" one Weibo user posting from the southern province of Hunan wrote.
Another user from Jiangsu province in east China, calling out the fact that Huang's resignation period coincided with ""GTA 6's"" release, wrote, ""Mystery solved: Jensen Huang quit his job to go home and play GTA 6. Now that he's had his fun, he's back at work.""
Others were quick to comment on his outfit. On his first day back at work, Huang had ditched his signature leather jacket, instead opting for a navy cotton one. Some Weibo users compared it to the jackets commonly worn by Chinese government officials, a style sometimes known as the ""cadre look.""
But other users adopted a more serious, analytical tone. One user, posting from China's eastern Zhejiang province, wrote that Huang's return would benefit the AI industry but could lead to tighter restrictions on China's access to AI technology.
Another user, posting from Shanxi province in northern China, wrote that Nvidia ""cannot function without Huang,"" speculating that the company's manufacturing partners had pressured its board to bring him back because they feared a replacement CEO could disrupt their operations.

designed by stanton williams with asif khan studio and julian harrap architects, the project brings a former center of trade back into public life, using adaptive reuse to connect london’s architectural heritage with its evolving urban identity.
  thomai tsimpou November 24, 2026 7 min        
 [FEATURED]
 
London Museum opens its new home inside Smithfield’s historic General Market on November 28th, 2026, following a decade-long transformation led by Stanton Williams, collaborating architect Asif Khan Studio and conservation architects Julian Harrap Architects. The project gives the Victorian market a new public role, preserving its historic character while opening the building to exhibitions, cultural events and everyday encounters with London’s history. The opening marks 50 years of London Museum and gives the historic Smithfield market a new lease of life as a place for Londoners to explore the past and present of their city.
 
Designed by Sir Horace Jones, the architect behind Tower Bridge and Leadenhall Market, the General Market opened in 1883 and operated for more than a century before closing in the 1990s. After decades of disuse, its restoration returns the building to public life, preserving its architectural character and introducing new functions across its expansive interiors. The transformation involves more than 70 trades and brings together heritage craftspeople, including master stonemasons, Welsh blacksmiths and the country’s last heritage coppersmiths.
 
 
 
The architects at Stanton Williams, in collaboration with Asif Khan Studio and Julian Harrap work with the existing features of the building. Original market signs, heritage shopfronts and decorative ironwork remain part of the experience, with the wrought-iron Phoenix columns continuing to support the enormous ceiling. The perimeter of historic shopfronts will accommodate independent organizations, extending the activities of the museum beyond its galleries. A pub and children’s arts charity, A New Direction, are among the first occupants, allowing different forms of public and community activity to coexist within the restored structure.
 
Sustainability is another significant component of the redevelopment. The project is targeting a BREEAM Outstanding rating, which would place it among the top one percent of buildings worldwide under the certification system. Supported by a loan from the Mayor of London’s Green Finance Fund, the ambition is to combine heritage conservation with the environmental requirements of a contemporary public building.
 
 
 
 Real Time, Our Time and Past Time form the three main areas of the museum. Real Time occupies West Poultry Avenue, a former covered street transformed into the main entrance of the building that introduces visitors to contemporary London through animated displays presenting changing urban data, from the city’s populations of pubs and bees to residents’ moods and listening habits.  Fragments of pottery and discarded Victorian clay pipes embedded in the floor introduce traces of earlier everyday life.
 
At the heart of the General Market, Our Time occupies the former trading floor of the Linbury Hall. It combines large-scale installations with a restaurant, shop and family-oriented show space. Its displays explore how Londoners gather and form communities, bringing together objects such as the Lord Mayor’s State Coach, the former Buckingham Palace switchboard and Syd’s Coffee Stall, an East End institution. The space also incorporates contemporary cultural references, including nightclub and shop signs, alongside a soundscape by electronic musician Scanner and a film by John Ogunmuyiwa exploring London’s crowds, from street celebrations to political demonstrations. The displays consider the ordinary places, sounds and encounters that contribute to the collective identity of the city.
 
Descending to Roman street level, Past Time presents 2,000 years of London’s urban history through objects, archival material and immersive installations. The collection moves from the city’s earliest inhabitants and Roman settlement to its development as a center of trade, imperial power, protest and contemporary culture. Highlights include the Cheapside Hoard, Roman writing tablets, Emmeline Pankhurst’s hunger strike medal and objects documenting London’s music, fashion and artistic scenes. A six-meter viewing window offers a direct view of Thameslink trains passing between Farringdon and City Thameslink. Set within the permanent galleries, it brings the city’s transport network into the museum, placing London’s history alongside the infrastructure that keeps it moving today.
 
 
 
 London Museum places particular emphasis on the participation of Londoners in shaping its new identity. More than 100,000 people, including schoolchildren, artists, writers, craftspeople and local communities, have contributed to the project. Their involvement extends into the collection, programming and interpretation, with displays incorporating contemporary voices alongside objects from the extensive historical holdings of the museum
 
 This approach is particularly visible in the museum’s changing program, which opens with London Tastes, an exploration of the capital’s food culture led by guest editors Ruby Tandoh and Jonathan Nunn. Running from November 2026 to August 2027, the program traces the city’s culinary identity through its markets, restaurants, independent businesses and neighborhood food traditions. The opening also forms part of a larger redevelopment of the Smithfield and Barbican area. The adjacent Poultry Market is scheduled to open in 2028, adding temporary exhibition spaces, a dedicated learning center and a collection store. The phased development will further expand the museum’s presence within the historic district.
 
 With a reported investment of £437 million and an ambition to welcome around two million visitors annually, the project represents a substantial commitment to cultural infrastructure in central London. Its architectural significance, however, lies less in the scale of the investment than in the decision to work with an existing urban landmark. By bringing the General Market back into public use, London Museum demonstrates how the city’s inherited buildings can accommodate new forms of cultural life while retaining traces of the activities that originally defined them.
 [FEATURED]

29,2026-11-24,"As the market bell rang out at the Nasdaq today, the Nvidia super-bounce faded.
Its shares slid and are down almost 30% over the past six months, well before the scandal involving Jensen Huang emerged earlier this month.
The legitimate fears about the staying power of the tech boom pre-dated Huang's exit. They have not gone away with his return.
Investors are jittery. Already Anthropic is now trading notably lower than last week's stock market flotation. The firm is the first purely AI company to be publicly listed, and is being seen as a big test for continued investor appetite for the technology.
President Trump's extraordinary phone call to CNBC taking credit for telling the board to reinstate Huang showed the extent of the operation - of some in Silicon Valley and within the US administration - to get him back at the helm.
Among the truly astonishing reflections from President Trump was a suggestion that a two-day investigation of allegations was done and had cleared Huang of national security concerns. 
The President said his remarkable move to intervene was because Huang is a ""national treasure"" who has the next generation of AI chips ""in his head"". Trump says this is why he needed to act. 
In markets, those sceptical about the AI boom say they have never seen an operation to prop up a stock market boom like this. The bond vigilantes and short sellers are now up against Silicon Valley and the White House.
Investors are trading not revenue numbers but levels of political support at a time when US voters have just delivered a backlash against AI data centres.
Against this backdrop, the recovery of the central cash-generating foundation of the tech boom - Nvidia and its $400bn (Â£304bn) annual sales of advanced chips - looks politically fragile.
This worsens other uncertainties and risks, such as Nvidia's central role in the web of financing, borrowing and cross-ownership to support every corner of the build out of data centres.  
The brief interim Nvidia chief executive Colette Kress had already signalled a rowing back from elements of this strategy last week. Where is that now?
The fall back in Anthropic's share price also raises questions about OpenAI's planned listing on the stock exchange. Ultimately these two companies might be the most critical part of all this. 
They remain valuable companies with phenomenal technology relied on by the world's biggest companies. The real question is whether that reliance eases, as corporations eschew expensive frontier models for cheaper generic models offered by China.
In turn, all this could be affected by the political backlash, to which the Nvidia psychodrama now contributes.
It is simply not normal to have trillion dollar swings in the value of individual companies.
The extraordinary volatility shows the fragile mood of investors.

				Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Tech > Mobile > Phones > Foldable Phones
For the tech enthusiast in your life, a foldable phone could be an interesting alternative to a traditional smartphone. We've rounded up our top picks, from book-style designs to flip phones.
Senior Technology Reporter
Senior Technology Reporter
November 24, 2026, 6:50 am ET
If you’re shopping for someone this holiday season who deserves something a little more special — and you’ve got the budget to spare — a foldable phone can be a unique and practical gift that your loved one will use long after the holidays are over. 
After reviewing our fair share of foldable phones, we’ve narrowed down our top three picks for the holidays and the perfect person on your list for each one. Who knows, you might end up caving in and treating yourself to a brand-new device? (You’ve been warned.) 
For more mobile gift ideas, from budget-friendly smartphones to wearable devices, be sure to check out the video below.
The iPhone Duo is far from the first foldable phone, but as Apple’s debut into the market, it’s generated plenty of buzz. The Duo’s superpower is its ability to integrate hardware and software, creating a seamless experience across both displays. 
The compact, 5.4-inch cover screen is great for sending texts and emails and checking notifications. The more expansive 7.6-inch inner display is ideal for multitasking, watching movies and working. It feels like using a smaller version of an iPad Mini that you can just fold up and toss in your pocket or bag.
The Duo is a great gift for someone who’s said they want a foldable phone, but refused to get one of the long-standing Android options already on the market. At last, Apple has its own offering that lets you stick with iOS. It’s a handy pick for people who often work on their phones and don’t always want to lug around a laptop. It’s also good for anyone who watches a lot of movies and TV shows on their mobile device. Plus, as a phone-tablet hybrid, it’s just fun to use. 
The iPhone Duo isn’t perfect. It doesn’t support Face ID, but Apple did recently add an option to unlock the phone using the front camera. This isn’t as secure as Face ID, so you’ll still need to use Touch ID or a passcode for more sensitive functions like using Apple Pay or accessing passwords, and after around 45 minutes of inactivity. 
The Duo’s cameras also aren’t as advanced as what you’ll get with the iPhone 18 Pro models, which have larger main-camera sensors and more advanced optical zoom capabilities. There’s also no telephoto camera on the Duo. Still, most people will be happy with the Duo’s 48-megapixel wide and ultrawide rear cameras, as well as the 12-megapixel selfie camera on the cover screen. The FaceTime camera on the inner display is best suited for video calls rather than snapping quality photos. 
Multitasking on the Duo is seamless, but it’s not as flexible as it is on other foldable phones. You can only have two apps open side by side, and you can’t readjust them. 
Perhaps the biggest downside is the price. The iPhone Duo starts at $1,999 for 256GB of storage. That’s why it’s a good idea to keep an eye out for any deals that can help lower the price.  
For the Android-lover in your life, there’s no better foldable pick than Samsung’s Galaxy Z Fold 8. Like the iPhone Duo, this phone has a passport-size design. (Technically, Samsung beat Apple to the punch, but let’s refrain from the “Who Did It First?” game for now.)
The Z Fold 8 has a 5.5-inch cover screen and a 7.6-inch inner display that’s great for watching videos, working and multitasking with up to three apps, which you can resize to your liking.
It’s also very thin, measuring 9.7mm thick when folded and 4.5mm thick when unfolded. That means it feels similar in thickness to a standard nonfolding phone, even when it’s closed. The Z Fold 8 fits squarely in a palm and easily slides into a pocket – tiny women’s pockets included.
Like the iPhone Duo, the Z Fold 8 doesn’t have a telephoto camera. But the 50-megapixel wide and ultrawide cameras on the back and the 10-megapixel selfie cameras do a solid job. 
The Z Fold 8 is also priced on the higher end, starting at $1,900 for 256GB. But its compact, sleek design and versatility help to make up for that sticker shock. Again, it’s a good idea to look out for any deals, especially around the holidays.
Some people prefer flip phones for their nostalgic designs and compact size. Motorola has plenty to choose from, but the best and most affordable pick for most people is the baseline 2026 Razr. 
The phone features a classic clamshell design and a spacious 3.6-inch cover screen, making it easy to check notifications, reply to texts, control music or follow navigation without opening the phone. The 6.9-inch inner display feels like using an average nonfolding phone, even if the screen is a little narrower and taller.
For most people, the Razr’s 50-megapixel wide and ultrawide cameras and 32-megapixel selfie camera on the inner display will do the trick. There’s no room for a telephoto camera, but the Razr pulls its weight for more casual photography. 
The Razr’s processor isn’t as powerful as what you’ll get on the pricier Razr Plus and Razr Ultra, but as long as you’re not playing intensive games all day, you probably won’t notice the difference. 
The Razr’s $800 starting price is significantly lower than the cost of book-style folding devices like the iPhone Duo and Galaxy Z Fold 8. The Razr could be a more appealing option for someone who doesn’t need a phone-tablet hybrid but just wants a phone that’s fun to use and taps into the nostalgia of the original Motorola Razr. 
No matter which phone you choose, your lucky recipient will enjoy the holiday spirit long after the season has passed, all while making quite the statement.

31,2026-11-24,"Huang’s ‘mistakes’ had consequences far beyond just chip giant Nvidia. He is back nonetheless, in less than two weeks after spy scandal sacking.
Save
Share
Jensen Huang has returned as Nvidia’s chief executive officer, just 11 days after he resigned from the board of the chip giant over an affair with an executive of a client company, alleged to be involved in a Chinese spy plot.
On Monday, Huang returned to a cheering crowd of employees at Nvidia’s headquarters in Santa Clara, California – a week after the board said it would not reinstate him.




US President Donald Trump took the credit for bringing back Huang – even as multiple inquiries continue, including an FBI-led counterintelligence review of his alleged links to a suspected Chinese intelligence asset.
The White House said: “We need to protect our geniuses.”
So, was Huang simply too big to fire? We look at what factors compelled Nvidia, the world’s most valuable semiconductor company, which controls 90 percent of the data centre graphics processing unit (GPU) market globally, to roll out the red carpet for Huang’s second coming.
Huang, the 63-year-old cofounder and chief of Nvidia, resigned on November 12 amid reports of extramarital affairs, including with Alice Huang, a Chinese businesswoman who led Singapore-based Megaspeed – and is believed to be a Chinese intelligence asset by some in the Trump administration. The New York Times first reported the alleged Chinese spy plot.
Beijing has denied that Alice Huang is a spy for China. Nvidia’s board began an investigation into the allegations, but Jensen Huang resigned before it concluded.
He was reportedly romantically involved with at least two other executives at Nvidia as well.
Analysts warned that losing Huang could significantly undermine Nvidia’s market value, with some estimating that his departure could wipe as much as 30 percent off the company’s roughly $4 trillion valuation. In the event, the share price fell by 27.7 percent to a November 20 low of $3.04 trillion.
Following news of his reinstatement, shares rebounded, propelling the company back to a valuation of $3.98 trillion at the close of trading on Monday.
Several investigations are under way, including a Justice Department antitrust probe into Nvidia’s business practices and an SEC inquiry into its accounting. Congress is also examining the potential diversion of Nvidia chips to China through Megaspeed.
Amid mounting pressure from the market and the Trump administration, Nvidia brought Huang back to lead the company by Monday.
“I am honoured to return to Nvidia and lead one of the most consequential technology companies in the world at the beginning of a new industrial revolution,” Huang said in his statement.
Huang’s departure prompted a backlash from influential figures in Silicon Valley.
A petition calling for his reinstatement attracted more than 180,000 signatures, including SpaceX CEO Elon Musk.
“Bring Jensen back while an independent investigation establishes the facts, unless and until you can provide compelling evidence that his removal is in NVIDIA’s best interests,” stated the letter published on the petition’s website.
But it was the Trump administration that made the decisive move, after a group of Silicon Valley insiders reportedly put together a so-called war room and lobbied the White House for support.
“The board decided to do what we wanted them to do,” Trump told CNBC in an interview, adding that he had spoken with board members and Huang.
“Basically, I was represented at the board,” Trump said, adding that Nvidia’s board asked for assurance about reported threats to national security.
“And we gave them assurances at least with everything we know and we know a lot,” the president said.
 

 
Huang does not have the voting power to control Nvidia’s board. In fact, he only has a 3.58 percent ownership stake – unlike, for example, Mark Zuckerberg, who leads Meta and holds a 13.4 percent economic stake, but commands nearly 60 percent of the company’s voting power.
Amid the campaign to reinstate Huang, the ousted CEO added that he would be “honoured” to ‌‌‌‌return if the board deemed it best.
Board Chair Harvey Jones, however, told investors that Huang left voluntarily and would not return.
The board announced a new committee to investigate the matter internally and also formed a so-called National Security and Export Control Committee to oversee trade compliance and national security risks.
Then came the about-face, with Jones saying on Monday that the board “believes Jensen is the right person to lead Nvidia and that his reinstatement is in the best interests of our stockholders, employees, partners and customers”.
“In the process of reaching its decision, the Board received input from senior levels of the United States government and based on that input the Board is confident that Mr Huang’s reinstatement poses no national security risk,” the statement added.
 

 
There are several reasons.
The US president’s administration, including Vice President JD Vance, pressured the board to chart a path for Huang’s return.
Trump claimed that he played a central role in Huang’s reinstatement to the consequential company, which had lacked a clear succession plan.
In the middle of the heated scandal, Trump repeatedly backed the “genius” Huang, who built Nvidia over three decades and was deeply involved in its technology strategy, relationships with customers and governments.
“I think this move is unprecedented in the sense that a sitting president has exerted very public pressure on a corporate board to make a particular decision,” Aleksandar Tomic, associate dean for strategy, innovation and technology at Boston College, told Al Jazeera.
Huang’s departure created concern among investors and employees about whether Nvidia could maintain its momentum without him – as reflected in its tumbling stock price.
Nvidia’s share price fell nearly 28 percent between November 11, when the board accepted Huang’s resignation, and November 20, amid mounting uncertainty over its leadership.
It surged 31 percent in early trading on Monday after the board announced his reinstatement – adding about a trillion dollars to the company’s valuation – lifting stock markets, with the tech-heavy Nasdaq climbing 6.5 percent and the S&P 500 by 4.75 percent.
Tech and AI companies make up about 40 percent of S&P 500 valuations.
On Tuesday, major Asian stock indices in Seoul, Tokyo and Taipei all rose as well.
In Taiwan, where Huang was born, the market soared by more than 9 percent, while South Korea’s Kospi index increased more than 6.8 percent and Japan’s Nikkei 225 by 3.8 percent.
Huang’s company makes chips that literally power the modern AI revolution as we see it.
Nvidia holds an extraordinary near-90 percent share of the market for GPUs – graphics processing units, which are specialised AI processors – used in AI data centres, giving it an oversized influence over the infrastructure powering the global AI boom.
That also makes the company central to the AI rivalry between the US and China.
Hours after Huang returned to Nvidia, the US added Megaspeed – the Singapore-based company at the centre of the alleged spying scandal involving Nvidia – to its trade blacklist.
Washington does not allow the sale of advanced chips to Beijing in order to maintain its lead in the rapidly developing industry and says it would punish those who try to circumvent the restrictions.
Nvidia’s customers include tech giants such as Microsoft, Google, Amazon and Meta, which rely on Nvidia hardware to build and operate their AI data centres.
Huang has spent years lobbying in Washington against such restrictions, arguing that restricting sales would surrender a strategically important market to domestic Chinese competitors. That puts him at odds with US legislators who view access to Nvidia’s advanced chips as a national security risk, particularly given their potential military applications.

						By Cult of Mac Deals • 4:20 pm, November 23, 2026

Tools like the Tykr stock screener make it easier to succeed in the wild world of investing, even if you’re a beginner. It rates thousands of stocks as On Sale, Watch or Overpriced, so you can find good investments — and avoid crummy ones.
Right now, you can save big on Tykr’s Premium plan. For a limited time, you can snag a three-year subscription for just $59.99. That’s an 80% discount off the regular price of $299.97.

The Tykr stock screener uses a rigorous algorithm to give each stock a score up to 20. The higher the score, the safer the investment. Who said picking stocks had to be complicated?

I’ll be thankful for quarterbacks this Thanksgiving. The matchups in Week 12 are just *chef's kiss*.
I don’t know about you, but I can’t wait to see Dak Prescott (fresh off a 50-point performance) opposite Jordan Love (fresh off a bye). That should be a barnburner.
But before we get ahead of ourselves, let’s get into Week 11 action.
Welcome back to QB Stock Market. These aren’t your typical quarterback rankings. I focus on contextualizing the fluctuations of starters' performances throughout the season. And every week, I’ll dive deeply into the four most compelling performers.
No one — not even Joe Burrow — is safe at the top. No one — not even Kyle McCord — is stuck at the bottom. There is room for quick ascent — and rapid decline. One question holds the most importance: What have you done for your team lately?
Previous: Offseason | Preseason | Week 1 | Week 2 | Week 3 | Week 4 | Week 5 | Week 6 | Week 7 | Week 8 | Week 9 | Week 10 | Week 11

This is the Joe Milton experience that I signed up for. He might’ve missed a deep opportunity to wideout Devonta Smith in the second half. But he was also firing in the first half. And he made a throw at the end to ice the game. I want more.

As tough as the year has been for Mayfield — and man, it has been ugly — he had a throw to Ted Hurst III that was as perfect as throws get. Mayfield dropped it into the corner of the end zone like he had nothing to lose, because well, he kinda doesn’t right now.

Over the past three weeks, he has thrown one touchdown and six interceptions. In the three weeks before that? He threw seven touchdowns and zero interceptions. 
Will the real Michael Penix Jr. please stand up?

Previous ranking: 21 ↓
This was not a fine performance from the young QB.
This wasn’t the type of game that a QB connoisseur studies and nods in approval.
I understand that the Giants pulled off a crazy upset win over the Jaguars, one of the best teams in the AFC. But McCarthy didn’t contribute much. It was his defense. McCarthy completed just 47.4% of his passes, 16.9% below expected, per Next Gen Stats. He missed multiple deep balls. He’s getting it done, I guess.
But!
McCarthy has a way of doing things well enough to keep his team in the mix. And given how good the Giants are as an entire team, there’s some value in the way their QB is playing. I’m not going to sell anyone false hope that the Giants will make the NFC Championship Game — or even win a playoff game. But McCarthy has ensured they’ll make the playoffs.
I actually want to highlight a tackle.
Giants running back Cam Skattebo surged forward on a carry in the third quarter, but the ball fired loose.
McCarthy ran from across the field to make a touchdown-saving tackle. And given that the Giants defense forced a turnover on downs on the ensuing drive, McCarthy’s tackle ended up being a 7-point play.
So, now, onto a throw. Just a week ago, I caused a little hubbub when I suggested that Vikings HC Kevin O’Connell must be thrilled to see Kirk Cousins layering the ball into the second level with so much finesse — after years of working with J.J. McCarthy, who never got a hang of the feathery throw that’s essential at the NFL level.
It’s about shaping the pass over the linebackers' heads while dropping it right into the hands of the receivers — often keeping them moving on a deep crossing pattern.
Well, I wouldn’t say McCarthy shaped his throw. But I will credit him (on the throw below) for layering the football and attacking the second level. McCarthy zipped the ball just over linebacker Foyesade Oluokun and into the hands of Malachi Fields with roughly three minutes left in a tight game. 
This throw wasn’t pretty. It wasn’t what O’Connell would have wanted. But I’ll bet you Giants HC John Harbaugh and OC Matt Nagy are happy with it.
McCarthy doesn’t play the position with grace. His throws sometimes look like Scottie Scheffler from the tee box — both athletes violently attempt to throw themselves out of their shoes. But McCarthy is getting the job done. The question is whether the nuance will ever arrive.
Previous ranking: 24 ↑
The Raiders went the trial-by-fire route with Mendoza. 
In his first start, he saw the Elizabeth Holmes-deceptive Seahawks defense. Which helped explain why he didn’t throw for his first touchdown until this second start. And given it came against the Broncos defense, a division rival and an elite defense, it was all the more impressive.
Mendoza found running back Ashton Jeanty near the right sideline as he went to the house for 68 yards. The playmaker and the playcaller (Raiders coach Klint Kubiak) did most of the work for Mendoza here, which is exactly how it should be when the young QB has to take on a Broncos defense with this much talent.
And while his TD wasn’t exactly a difficult throw, he did manage to zip a few balls into tight coverage and tight windows. My favorite? A throw to receiver Jack Bech. Mendoza was rolling right and could feel pressure coming toward him on the front side. So he stopped, set his feet and ripped a back-shoulder pass to Bech over the middle.
It was a gusty throw — and placed perfectly. 
But there were problems.
It’s one thing to make good throws and avoid game-wrecking mistakes. It’s another thing to show up at the money moments. And it looks like Mendoza made a series of mistakes in the fourth quarter. On a third down early in the fourth quarter, Mendoza failed to convert a first down when he rushed the ball — and he might have also made a mistake with his pre-snap read, likely dictating he throw to Jeanty (rather than go ahead with a QB keeper). 
But that set up a fourth-and-2 where Mendoza simply air-mailed the ball to his receiver along the sideline. The same thing happened again on a third-and-10, with Jalen Nailor creating enough separation for a pinpoint passing window. Mendoza thew an uncatchable ball high and outside.
Against that defense and at this point in his career, Mendoza gets a pass.
It’s easy to look at what he did right and feel optimistic. But with any youngster, it’ll be about avoiding repeated mistakes. So let’s see him clean these issues up.
Previous ranking: 20
Ward doesn’t have enough weapons to duel with a team such as the Cowboys. Nor does he have the defense. But he’s stringing together respectable performances where he is seeing the game quicker and clearer. This was another one like that.
Previous ranking: 14 ↓
Tom Brady did not mince words in breaking down Shough’s game against the Bears. Brady felt like Shough wasn’t seeing the field well — missing openings in the play designs both with poor timing and misreads. The Bears' defense is really good. But Shough missed multiple opportunities to punish them.

Previous ranking: 5 ↓
Sometimes, you just wake up on the wrong side of the bed. Sometimes, life gives you lemons, and you can’t remember how to make lemonade. Sometimes, you throw a touchdown, and the officials tell you it's an incompletion. Lawrence had one of those days.
Previous ranking: 4 ↓
Allen has 15 big-time throws this year at the clip of 4.5%, the lowest percentage since 2019.
Allen also has 18 turnover-worthy plays at the rate of 4.7%, the highest percentage since 2018.
It’s one of the worst seasons for him as a passer when it comes to taking risks, with 14 passing touchdowns and nine interceptions — including two picks against the Dolphins in Week 11. The thing about Miami, however, is that it's in the top 10 in passing yards allowed. So its defense is not a pushover.
But of course, if this were an MVP season from Allen, he would light them up anyway. And he couldn’t. Which has been the case for quite some time. He’s having a really good season. He’s just not putting together a compelling case for MVP.
The hidden production, of course, is on the ground, where Allen has added 11 touchdowns. That puts him at 25 total touchdowns — not far from league leader Joe Burrow and runner-up Dak Prescott. And it puts Allen even with Brock Purdy and ahead of Jared Goff and Patrick Mahomes.
In just about every way, Allen is playing the QB position in a way that’s atypical — even for him. I don’t want to compare it to Jalen Hurts. But I don’t not want to compare it to Hurts. 
I don’t want to go overboard in praising Allen. But I do think he has been more impressive than people realize. He remains the biggest reason the Bills are clear favorites in the AFC.
Previous ranking: 10 ↑
In Week 8, Goff took down the Vikings, the league’s best defense — with help from Jahmyr Gibbs. And this week, Goff ripped up the sixth-best defense, the Buccaneers. He was 20 of 28 for 279 yards and two touchdowns.
Previous ranking: 2 ↓
When you play the Cardinals, you gotta put up at least 300 passing yards and three TDs. Which is exactly what Mahomes did.
Previous ranking: 1 ↓
Twenty-nine touchdown passes and 3,095 passing yards. That would normally keep a QB safe and sound at the top of the list. Alas …
Previous ranking: 3 ↑
I don’t really care who has thrown more touchdowns or who has the most passing yards. 
There is no quarterback with more control of his offense than Purdy. He is the picture of command. Even with that baby face. Even at 6-foot-1, 210 pounds.
This past Sunday night, Purdy exposed the NFL’s best defense — the unit that was basically unreadable for every quarterback this year. Brian Flores and the Vikings have been such a difficult matchup for some of the league’s best QBs. The Packers' Jordan Love got flattened. The Bears' Caleb Williams looked lost. Purdy dissected the Vikings with poise that surpassed even what Bills QB Josh Allen demonstrated in his matchup against Minnesota.
Against the blitz, Purdy was 16 of 18 for 195 yards and two touchdowns. 
He got the ball out quickly, because 49ers coach Kyle Shanahan gave his QB answers. But it takes a smart, quick-thinking passer to simply get the ball out on time against this Minnesota defense. Purdy didn’t panic when the Vikings sent one blitz after another. Instead, he looked for the opportunities that the blitz offered, with vacancies downfield for his pass-catchers to exploit. And man, did they exploit those opportunities.
There might not have been a better example than when the 49ers basically lined up 6-foot-5 Evans in the slot and sent him vertical against the blitz, where he split the safeties for a massive chunk play. And Purdy got the ball off with a blitzer diving at him after roughly two seconds.
Purdy isn’t airing the ball out with crazy deep passes like Love and Joe Burrow. He’s not firing the ball around the field like Allen, Williams and Patrick Mahomes. But he’s the best QB in the NFL right now, because he does every little thing well.

47,2026-11-24,"For years, accusations of insider trading have targeted politicians and celebrities. But with the second administration of U.S. President Donald Trump coinciding with the rise of online prediction markets such as Polymarket and Kalshi, rumors have multiplied that the president and his inside circle have gambled using privileged information before it's public, a practice that has yet to be fully outlawed.
In November, Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang, who admitted to several affairs, including one with a suspected Chinese spy, was removed by the company's board, then quickly reinstated. Trump said he intervened in Huang's favor.
This resulted in reports that Trump, who owns Nvidia shares, financially benefited from Huang's return. Some critics suspected Trump even traded on this information. This would constitute insider trading, which is illegal, according to the Securities and Exchange Commission.
