90,2026-11-24,"
michaelharley.net
sysop: michael · est. 2012



Michael Harley


November 01, 2026

        5 min read
      
In the evenings after work, I will often sit out back on the porch and do some phone rotting. That usually takes the form of me just flipping through reels on Instagram, allowing the algorithm to take me where it may. I usually pair this with a healthy tumbler of rum or bourbon. I'm turning my brain off after work, and I'm just having a good time on the internet. The kids might call it dopamine maxxing? Hell, I don't know. Who gives a shit about what those assholes would call it?
Yes yes, I'm aware. Instagram is evil. Down with Zuckerberg and all the rest. waves pitchfork around
Anywho, I often encounter songs I really really like, and I would like to listen and discover more of please. Instagram makes it hard for me to connect with the band outside of their platform. I was trying to make this connection manually myself at first. I would just email myself a reel from my phone. Then when I'm back on the sofa and in the mood to track down new music, I'd open the reels and do my best to search on Bandcamp or YouTube or try to figure out if some of these musicians had a way to buy music from them. Very few do these days. They send you their Spotify link or send you to their TikTok page. I don't want to do any of that. I'd like to trade US dollars in exchange for some music files I can download onto my computer, where I can listen as much as I like on any device I own thank you please. Retro maxxing.
So, I used Claude Code to build a skill that does this for me. It opens the Instagram reel and attempts to identify the artist and song. It then tries to determine if the artists have a Bandcamp page so I can purchase the songs I want, and if it finds one, it gives me the URL so I can click it to complete the trade I want. I want to buy music from artists who make music I like. That's a fair and wholesome endeavor, I feel, but this recently stopped working.
It turns out Bandcamp added a challenge on their search API page. Ah bummer. No worries, that was an unpublished workaround anyway. As I was mulling over this discovery, the robot went on to explain that it would not help me work around the challenge. cue Nick Young question marks
Well, that led me down a whole nother rabbit hole, which is turning into this post. There's another branch of my skill that attempts to locate the same song from the Instagram reel on YouTube, and it'll download the song to my computer if I ask.
So let me get this straight. The robot, which is my robot that I pay a pretty penny for, will not attempt to work around the challenge at Bandcamp so that I can more easily connect with bands to complete a sale, but it will download the YouTube song, which doesn't send any money to the band or anyone???
This is the real danger of the frontier AI models. Not that it won't help me hack Bandcamp[1], but that this model doesn't really work for us. Well, they do, but only insofar as Anthropic or OpenAI or whoever else allows them to. In this case, Anthropic is trying to ensure that people can't use their models for evil. I'm not trying to do evil. I'm actually trying to do good! I want to buy something from a small artist on the internet. That's basically the most wholesome thing a person can do on the internet!
Have no doubt, dear reader, these companies want to dominate. They want to capture the market. They want all the money. It'll be the same march to enshittification that happened with Google. A bunch of nerds who built something cool sold out, and it turned into just another business that decided to remove its don't be evil mantra.
Right now, I think the robots are providing real information to the best of their ability, but I think it's only a matter of time until we must wonder if the answers they're providing are real answers that the robot found, or if they're weighted answers Anthropic put there because a company gave it enough money for product placement. I want to know the best robot vacuum according to old reddit or other trusted sources like Wirecutter, weighed and measured according to what I, and thus my robot agent, think are important requirements. I don't want to know which robot vacuum parent multinational conglomerate has a large ad budget.
And it's not just ads. If Anthropic can tell its model not to work around a challenge, then it can tell its model to do whatever the US government tells it to do, including not helping people download copyrighted music. It does that some now, but it's easy to slip the noose on that. For now.
This is the reason I want a local model that runs in my homelab. My robot should work for me and have my best interests in mind, not the interests of US corporate copyright holders. I plan to study up on what ""open models"" even means. I've been thinking about these as the FOSS version of LLMs, but I think that doesn't exactly map, as the biggest piece seems to be how the model was trained. The Chinese models are likely to refuse to answer questions about Taiwan, for example.
On the existential threat or tool question, I'm firmly in the tool camp, and I really do not like artificial restrictions on my tools. I will do everything I reasonably can to remove those restrictions.
And to be clear, I'm not trying to work around Bandcamp's challenge. I'm responding to the fact that Claude preemptively told me it wouldn't help me do this. ↩︎

91,2026-11-24,"Anthropic's historic IPO drew crowds to Times Square, but downtown on Wall Street, investors are a little less enthusiastic as the stock struggles days after the hugely hyped offering.
The newly minted stock fell as much as 11% on Tuesday to $75.61 before closing the day down 6.5% at $81.90. That marks a decline of 9% from the $83 IPO price, and a drop of 29% from the high of $115.10 when the stock debuted on November 19.
It's a sobering streak of declines for a stock that was so massively hyped, and the losses have sparked a debate about what's gone wrong and what the future might hold for shares of the Claude maker.
Here's what market pros are saying about Anthropic stock as it struggles in the days after the IPO.
For Greg Halter, director of research at the Carnegie Investment Counsel, which oversees $7 billion in assets, Anthropic's post-IPO fall isn't surprising. In his view, it's par for the course for splashy IPOs.
""For long-term investors, there is often little reason to chase a stock immediately following its public debut,"" Halter told Business Insider. ""The initial enthusiasm surrounding a highly anticipated IPO can take several months to settle. Anthropic popped at the open, giving us reason to believe there may be a better entry point in the future.""
Melissa Otto, the global head of visible alpha research at S&P Global, said that Anthropic's volatility is likely to persist until the company's first earnings report.
""The market likely wants to understand the visibility of Anthropic's sustained revenue growth and the road to profitability,"" she said. ""At the moment, there is concern about the AI trade overall, due to the high levels of expected capex spending.""
She added that if high AI capex expectations from hyperscalers are pushed down, that could also weigh on Anthropic in the coming months.

                                  Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.
                                
eToro analyst Bret Kenwell sees a clear reminder for investors following Anthropic's poor start to the week.
""Enthusiasm for AI is not a blank check for every AI stock,"" he said, emphasizing the more selective nature in which some investors are approaching the AI trade and aren't willing to buy an unproven stock simply because the company is a high-profile name in the market.
""What happens next will depend less on the broader AI trade and more on Anthropic's own results,"" Kenwell said. ""Investors will want to see customer demand keep growing, compute costs become more manageable and a clearer path to profitability.""
Luke Lango, chief technology analyst at InvestorPlace, sees Anthropic's struggles as problem with the AI trade rather than the Claude maker itself. He acknowledged, though, that market conditions are working against it in a way that cannot be ignored.
""It's the best company in AI,"" he said, of Anthropic. ""But right now the market is selling the whole sector and nothing gets spared. Add a hot-IPO hangover, a big wall of insider stock waiting to hit the market and a trillion-dollar-plus price tag, and you get a quick round trip back to and even below the IPO price.""
He added that the AI buildout isn't stopping, but it is slowing down. That means Anthropic will likely need to fall further before it starts rising again once a rotation back into the AI trade kicks off.
According to Zacks Investment Management market strategist Brian Mulberry, investors may be feeling some fatigue as large amounts of capital flood the market. This is a factor that some investors expressed concern about prior to the roster of mega-IPOs beginning with SpaceX earlier this year.
That doesn't mean Mulberry is bearish on Anthropic, but he's cautious amid all of the volatility.
""Demand for AI tools is still very strong and that feeds a positive narrative back into ANT, meaning their AI tools should continue to monetize at a steady pace,"" he wrote. ""But it's the road to profitability that seems to change day to day as spending commitments remain very large.""

By Victor Tangermann



					Published				

					Nov 24, 2026 12:46 PM EST				

Can’t-miss innovations from the bleeding edge of science and tech
It was the whiplash-inducing market swing of all market swings.
Once Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang announced that he was leaving the company on November 12, all hell broke loose. The company’s shares had already been showing signs of stress over the previous months, and his resignation was the straw that broke the camel’s back, sending the stock on an even steeper downward trajectory — and taking the entire NASDAQ along with it by the end of last week.
Then his baffling reinstatement on Monday had the opposite effect. Jubilant investors poured their funds back into the AI chipmaker, sending it soaring by over 30 percent and adding a full $1 trillion back to the company’s market cap.
But that’s not quite where the story ends. As the scandal metastasizes — president Donald Trump’s personal efforts to intervene on Huang’s behalf did nothing to address furious lawmakers’ burning questions — Nvidia’s shares are now plummeting once more.
As if investors woke up with a bad hangover, shares were down nearly seven percent by midday on Tuesday, indicating plenty of residual nerves that survived this week’s historic upswing. At a hair over $150, that puts the company’s stock price roughly at the point where the stock was in the days following Huang’s resignation.
There are numerous factors at play. Huang may be back at the helm of the embattled company, but ongoing government investigations remain unaffected by the development. For one, Nvidia’s accounting is being scrutinized by the Securities and Exchange Commission, as Reuters reported last week.
Huang will also be appearing twice before Congress in December alone and will face a barrage of questions from lawmakers about the company’s circumventing of export controls to China — efforts that were being directly facilitated by the Trump administration.
That’s not to mention Huang’s massively tarnished reputation, turning him from the AI industry’s undisputed leader into a major legal and business liability, not to mention a widely-reviled sex pest who sleeps with his insubordinates.
While Trump insists that he has “no reason to believe” that Huang’s reinstatement poses any risks to national security, his massive financial conflict of interest undermines anything he has to offer on the matter.
Analysts are now wary of all those liabilities, which could further imperil the company.
“With all the scrutiny they’re going to be under, it’s going to be very difficult for them to have the same level of deal-making capacity out there in the marketplace,” Questar Capital Partners chief investment officer Richard Reyle told CNBC.
The board’s abrupt reversal in less than a week to unanimously vote for Huang’s reinstatement also remains a major sticking point, undermining its ability to meaningfully steer the ship.
The board faced “legitimate questions as to their judgment and process, which has to make one wonder how they come to their other important decisions,” Bernstein managing director Stacy Rasgon told CNBC.
More on Nvidia: Nvidia Decides That Profits Are More Important Than Not Sleeping With Subordinates, Reinstates Jensen Huang as CEO and Board Director

Harvey Jones will remain chair of Nvidia's board, according to the company. The announcement was issued before U.S. markets opened Monday and reversed a resignation that had erased hundreds of billions of dollars in market value and unsettled the artificial intelligence (AI) trade, according to the report. MarketWatch reported that the stock closed up $39.52, or 31.48%, at $165.05, its biggest percentage gain in 23 years.Board Cites Discussions With Huang, U.S. Government Input Nvidia's board said it held a series of discussions with Huang and was satisfied with his candor and his ability to lead the company. Jones said in a statement: ""The board believes Jensen is the right person to lead Nvidia, and that his reinstatement is in the best interests of our stockholders, employees, partners and customers."" Jones said the board had every confidence in Huang and was grateful to Kress for her service as interim CEO. The company said the board received input from senior levels of the U.S. government and, based on that input, does not believe Huang's reinstatement poses a national security risk. The reversal followed a different message delivered only days earlier. Just last week, Jones told investors during the company's third-quarter earnings call that Nvidia had no plans to bring back Huang, according to the report. The company's position changed after the board's discussions with Huang and the government input, the report stated.Resignation Followed Allegations Involving Alice Huang, Employees Huang resigned from Nvidia on Nov. 12 after an alleged affair with Alice Huang (unrelated), who is suspected of being a Chinese spy, according to the report. Huang admitted to having an affair at the time but did not name the woman, the report stated. He also allegedly had affairs with two Nvidia employees, including executive Kimberly Powell. Alice was the executive of Singapore-based Megaspeed, which is suspected of smuggling high-powered Nvidia chips to China, the report said. The allegations around the company's chip exports sit within a broader set of U.S. export controls on advanced AI processors. The U.S. Department of Commerce issued updated guidance restricting Nvidia's sales of advanced AI chips to China, targeting the company's most sophisticated processors including the Blackwell series [2]. The resignation also landed amid scrutiny of Nvidia's China business more broadly. U.S. lawmakers have accused Nvidia of aiding China's military, alleging that chips sold to Chinese AI firm DeepSeek ended up supporting a model used by the People's Liberation Army [3]. Nvidia's critics in Congress have demanded strict export enforcement, even if it means halting sales [3].Huang, Trump Issue Statements on Reinstatement Huang said in a statement, ""I am honored to return to Nvidia and lead one of the most consequential technology companies in the world at the beginning of a new industrial revolution."" Huang said he was grateful to Jones, Kress, the executive staff, employees, shareholders, industry leaders, partners and his family. President Trump said in a Truth Social post that the country needs Huang's leadership at Nvidia to ensure the success and prosperity of the company and the American economy. Trump said the U.S. government has no reason to believe Huang's return poses a national security risk and that the U.S. attorney general confirmed the government does not possess evidence Huang engaged in unlawful conduct. Trump called Huang a ""Great Patriot and National Treasure,"" according to the post. Huang's allies, including Trump, reportedly lobbied for his return as CEO. The political context around Nvidia's China policy has been unsettled for more than a year. The Trump administration reversed a policy to allow Nvidia to sell its most advanced AI chips, the H200 GPUs, to China, drawing bipartisan concern in Congress over national security [4]. Earlier, the administration had reversed a ban and allowed Nvidia to sell AI chips to China after lobbying and a meeting between Huang and Trump [5].Related Stocks Climb; Board Cites Stockholder Interests Shares of neoclouds CoreWeave and Nebius also climbed more than 30% Monday, according to the report. The wider market moved higher as well. The Nasdaq surged 6.51%, closing at 24,089.56 points, and the S&P 500 rose 4.73%, according to Just the News [6]. Nvidia's board said Huang's reinstatement is in the best interests of stockholders, employees, partners and customers. Jones said, ""We have every confidence in Jensen and look forward to working with him as he resumes his leadership of the Company."" 247WallSt reported that Nvidia surged 27% after the board reinstated Huang as CEO, adding about $828 billion in value [7]. The company Huang returns to remains the dominant supplier of AI accelerators. Nvidia holds a 94% market share in GPU sales, and the company reported a 56% year-over-year revenue increase driven by AI and data-intensive applications [8]. Its newest Blackwell platform drew the CEO's own description of demand: Huang said sales of the Blackwell platform are ""off the charts"" and that ""cloud GPUs are sold out"" [9]. Nvidia also agreed on Sept. 2 to acquire Hugging Face Inc., an open platform for AI developers, for $12.9 billion, according to a company statement [10].Conclusion The reinstatement caps a 12-day period in which Nvidia's leadership passed to an interim chief executive and back, with the board citing discussions with Huang and input from senior U.S. government officials as the basis for its decision.Â Whether the underlying allegations involving Alice Huang and the Megaspeed export questions are resolved in the public record remains a separate matter from the board's decision, which the company framed around stockholder interests and national security input.References   Nvidia stock has best day since May 2003, gaining nearly $1T in market cap after Jensen Huang is reinstated as CEO. https://finance.yahoo.com/technology/article/nvidia-stock-has-best-day-since-may-2003-gaining-nearly-1t-in-market-cap-after-jensen-huang-is-reinstated-as-ceo-212735744.html  Nvidia AI Chip Sales to China Face New U.S. Export Restrictions - NaturalNews.com, July 01, 2026. Nvidia also agreed on Sept. 2 to acquire Hugging Face Inc., an open platform for AI developers, for $12.9 billion, according to a company statement [10].Conclusion The reinstatement caps a 12-day period in which Nvidia's leadership passed to an interim chief executive and back, with the board citing discussions with Huang and input from senior U.S. government officials as the basis for its decision.Â Whether the underlying allegations involving Alice Huang and the Megaspeed export questions are resolved in the public record remains a separate matter from the board's decision, which the company framed around stockholder interests and national security input.References   Nvidia stock has best day since May 2003, gaining nearly $1T in market cap after Jensen Huang is reinstated as CEO. https://finance.yahoo.com/technology/article/nvidia-stock-has-best-day-since-may-2003-gaining-nearly-1t-in-market-cap-after-jensen-huang-is-reinstated-as-ceo-212735744.html  Nvidia AI Chip Sales to China Face New U.S. Export Restrictions - NaturalNews.com, July 01, 2026. Nvidia's board said it held a series of discussions with Huang and was satisfied with his candor and his ability to lead the company. Jones said in a statement: ""The board believes Jensen is the right person to lead Nvidia, and that his reinstatement is in the best interests of our stockholders, employees, partners and customers."" Jones said the board had every confidence in Huang and was grateful to Kress for her service as interim CEO. The company said the board received input from senior levels of the U.S. government and, based on that input, does not believe Huang's reinstatement poses a national security risk. The reversal followed a different message delivered only days earlier. Just last week, Jones told investors during the company's third-quarter earnings call that Nvidia had no plans to bring back Huang, according to the report. The company's position changed after the board's discussions with Huang and the government input, the report stated.Resignation Followed Allegations Involving Alice Huang, Employees Huang resigned from Nvidia on Nov. 12 after an alleged affair with Alice Huang (unrelated), who is suspected of being a Chinese spy, according to the report. Huang admitted to having an affair at the time but did not name the woman, the report stated. He also allegedly had affairs with two Nvidia employees, including executive Kimberly Powell. Alice was the executive of Singapore-based Megaspeed, which is suspected of smuggling high-powered Nvidia chips to China, the report said. The allegations around the company's chip exports sit within a broader set of U.S. export controls on advanced AI processors. The U.S. Department of Commerce issued updated guidance restricting Nvidia's sales of advanced AI chips to China, targeting the company's most sophisticated processors including the Blackwell series [2]. The resignation also landed amid scrutiny of Nvidia's China business more broadly. U.S. lawmakers have accused Nvidia of aiding China's military, alleging that chips sold to Chinese AI firm DeepSeek ended up supporting a model used by the People's Liberation Army [3]. Nvidia's critics in Congress have demanded strict export enforcement, even if it means halting sales [3].Huang, Trump Issue Statements on Reinstatement Huang said in a statement, ""I am honored to return to Nvidia and lead one of the most consequential technology companies in the world at the beginning of a new industrial revolution."" Huang said he was grateful to Jones, Kress, the executive staff, employees, shareholders, industry leaders, partners and his family. President Trump said in a Truth Social post that the country needs Huang's leadership at Nvidia to ensure the success and prosperity of the company and the American economy. Trump said the U.S. government has no reason to believe Huang's return poses a national security risk and that the U.S. attorney general confirmed the government does not possess evidence Huang engaged in unlawful conduct. Trump called Huang a ""Great Patriot and National Treasure,"" according to the post. Huang's allies, including Trump, reportedly lobbied for his return as CEO. The political context around Nvidia's China policy has been unsettled for more than a year. The Trump administration reversed a policy to allow Nvidia to sell its most advanced AI chips, the H200 GPUs, to China, drawing bipartisan concern in Congress over national security [4]. Earlier, the administration had reversed a ban and allowed Nvidia to sell AI chips to China after lobbying and a meeting between Huang and Trump [5].Related Stocks Climb; Board Cites Stockholder Interests Shares of neoclouds CoreWeave and Nebius also climbed more than 30% Monday, according to the report. The wider market moved higher as well. The Nasdaq surged 6.51%, closing at 24,089.56 points, and the S&P 500 rose 4.73%, according to Just the News [6]. Nvidia's board said Huang's reinstatement is in the best interests of stockholders, employees, partners and customers. Jones said, ""We have every confidence in Jensen and look forward to working with him as he resumes his leadership of the Company."" 247WallSt reported that Nvidia surged 27% after the board reinstated Huang as CEO, adding about $828 billion in value [7]. The company Huang returns to remains the dominant supplier of AI accelerators. Nvidia holds a 94% market share in GPU sales, and the company reported a 56% year-over-year revenue increase driven by AI and data-intensive applications [8]. Its newest Blackwell platform drew the CEO's own description of demand: Huang said sales of the Blackwell platform are ""off the charts"" and that ""cloud GPUs are sold out"" [9]. Nvidia also agreed on Sept. 2 to acquire Hugging Face Inc., an open platform for AI developers, for $12.9 billion, according to a company statement [10].Conclusion The reinstatement caps a 12-day period in which Nvidia's leadership passed to an interim chief executive and back, with the board citing discussions with Huang and input from senior U.S. government officials as the basis for its decision.Â Whether the underlying allegations involving Alice Huang and the Megaspeed export questions are resolved in the public record remains a separate matter from the board's decision, which the company framed around stockholder interests and national security input.References   Nvidia stock has best day since May 2003, gaining nearly $1T in market cap after Jensen Huang is reinstated as CEO. https://finance.yahoo.com/technology/article/nvidia-stock-has-best-day-since-may-2003-gaining-nearly-1t-in-market-cap-after-jensen-huang-is-reinstated-as-ceo-212735744.html  Nvidia AI Chip Sales to China Face New U.S. Export Restrictions - NaturalNews.com, July 01, 2026. Nvidia also agreed on Sept. 2 to acquire Hugging Face Inc., an open platform for AI developers, for $12.9 billion, according to a company statement [10].Conclusion The reinstatement caps a 12-day period in which Nvidia's leadership passed to an interim chief executive and back, with the board citing discussions with Huang and input from senior U.S. government officials as the basis for its decision.Â Whether the underlying allegations involving Alice Huang and the Megaspeed export questions are resolved in the public record remains a separate matter from the board's decision, which the company framed around stockholder interests and national security input.References   Nvidia stock has best day since May 2003, gaining nearly $1T in market cap after Jensen Huang is reinstated as CEO. https://finance.yahoo.com/technology/article/nvidia-stock-has-best-day-since-may-2003-gaining-nearly-1t-in-market-cap-after-jensen-huang-is-reinstated-as-ceo-212735744.html  Nvidia AI Chip Sales to China Face New U.S. Export Restrictions - NaturalNews.com, July 01, 2026. Resignation Followed Allegations Involving Alice Huang, Employees Huang resigned from Nvidia on Nov. 12 after an alleged affair with Alice Huang (unrelated), who is suspected of being a Chinese spy, according to the report. Huang admitted to having an affair at the time but did not name the woman, the report stated. He also allegedly had affairs with two Nvidia employees, including executive Kimberly Powell. Alice was the executive of Singapore-based Megaspeed, which is suspected of smuggling high-powered Nvidia chips to China, the report said. The allegations around the company's chip exports sit within a broader set of U.S. export controls on advanced AI processors. The U.S. Department of Commerce issued updated guidance restricting Nvidia's sales of advanced AI chips to China, targeting the company's most sophisticated processors including the Blackwell series [2]. The resignation also landed amid scrutiny of Nvidia's China business more broadly. U.S. lawmakers have accused Nvidia of aiding China's military, alleging that chips sold to Chinese AI firm DeepSeek ended up supporting a model used by the People's Liberation Army [3]. Nvidia's critics in Congress have demanded strict export enforcement, even if it means halting sales [3].Huang, Trump Issue Statements on Reinstatement Huang said in a statement, ""I am honored to return to Nvidia and lead one of the most consequential technology companies in the world at the beginning of a new industrial revolution."" Huang said he was grateful to Jones, Kress, the executive staff, employees, shareholders, industry leaders, partners and his family. President Trump said in a Truth Social post that the country needs Huang's leadership at Nvidia to ensure the success and prosperity of the company and the American economy. Trump said the U.S. government has no reason to believe Huang's return poses a national security risk and that the U.S. attorney general confirmed the government does not possess evidence Huang engaged in unlawful conduct. Trump called Huang a ""Great Patriot and National Treasure,"" according to the post. Huang's allies, including Trump, reportedly lobbied for his return as CEO. The political context around Nvidia's China policy has been unsettled for more than a year. The Trump administration reversed a policy to allow Nvidia to sell its most advanced AI chips, the H200 GPUs, to China, drawing bipartisan concern in Congress over national security [4]. Earlier, the administration had reversed a ban and allowed Nvidia to sell AI chips to China after lobbying and a meeting between Huang and Trump [5].Related Stocks Climb; Board Cites Stockholder Interests Shares of neoclouds CoreWeave and Nebius also climbed more than 30% Monday, according to the report. The wider market moved higher as well. The Nasdaq surged 6.51%, closing at 24,089.56 points, and the S&P 500 rose 4.73%, according to Just the News [6]. Nvidia's board said Huang's reinstatement is in the best interests of stockholders, employees, partners and customers. Jones said, ""We have every confidence in Jensen and look forward to working with him as he resumes his leadership of the Company."" 247WallSt reported that Nvidia surged 27% after the board reinstated Huang as CEO, adding about $828 billion in value [7]. The company Huang returns to remains the dominant supplier of AI accelerators. Nvidia holds a 94% market share in GPU sales, and the company reported a 56% year-over-year revenue increase driven by AI and data-intensive applications [8]. Its newest Blackwell platform drew the CEO's own description of demand: Huang said sales of the Blackwell platform are ""off the charts"" and that ""cloud GPUs are sold out"" [9]. Nvidia also agreed on Sept. 2 to acquire Hugging Face Inc., an open platform for AI developers, for $12.9 billion, according to a company statement [10].Conclusion The reinstatement caps a 12-day period in which Nvidia's leadership passed to an interim chief executive and back, with the board citing discussions with Huang and input from senior U.S. government officials as the basis for its decision.Â Whether the underlying allegations involving Alice Huang and the Megaspeed export questions are resolved in the public record remains a separate matter from the board's decision, which the company framed around stockholder interests and national security input.References   Nvidia stock has best day since May 2003, gaining nearly $1T in market cap after Jensen Huang is reinstated as CEO. https://finance.yahoo.com/technology/article/nvidia-stock-has-best-day-since-may-2003-gaining-nearly-1t-in-market-cap-after-jensen-huang-is-reinstated-as-ceo-212735744.html  Nvidia AI Chip Sales to China Face New U.S. Export Restrictions - NaturalNews.com, July 01, 2026. Jones said, ""We have every confidence in Jensen and look forward to working with him as he resumes his leadership of the Company."" 247WallSt reported that Nvidia surged 27% after the board reinstated Huang as CEO, adding about $828 billion in value [7]. The company Huang returns to remains the dominant supplier of AI accelerators. Nvidia holds a 94% market share in GPU sales, and the company reported a 56% year-over-year revenue increase driven by AI and data-intensive applications [8]. Its newest Blackwell platform drew the CEO's own description of demand: Huang said sales of the Blackwell platform are ""off the charts"" and that ""cloud GPUs are sold out"" [9]. Nvidia also agreed on Sept. 2 to acquire Hugging Face Inc., an open platform for AI developers, for $12.9 billion, according to a company statement [10].Conclusion The reinstatement caps a 12-day period in which Nvidia's leadership passed to an interim chief executive and back, with the board citing discussions with Huang and input from senior U.S. government officials as the basis for its decision.Â Whether the underlying allegations involving Alice Huang and the Megaspeed export questions are resolved in the public record remains a separate matter from the board's decision, which the company framed around stockholder interests and national security input.References   Nvidia stock has best day since May 2003, gaining nearly $1T in market cap after Jensen Huang is reinstated as CEO. https://finance.yahoo.com/technology/article/nvidia-stock-has-best-day-since-may-2003-gaining-nearly-1t-in-market-cap-after-jensen-huang-is-reinstated-as-ceo-212735744.html  Nvidia AI Chip Sales to China Face New U.S. Export Restrictions - NaturalNews.com, July 01, 2026. Huang, Trump Issue Statements on Reinstatement Huang said in a statement, ""I am honored to return to Nvidia and lead one of the most consequential technology companies in the world at the beginning of a new industrial revolution."" Huang said he was grateful to Jones, Kress, the executive staff, employees, shareholders, industry leaders, partners and his family. President Trump said in a Truth Social post that the country needs Huang's leadership at Nvidia to ensure the success and prosperity of the company and the American economy. Trump said the U.S. government has no reason to believe Huang's return poses a national security risk and that the U.S. attorney general confirmed the government does not possess evidence Huang engaged in unlawful conduct. Trump called Huang a ""Great Patriot and National Treasure,"" according to the post. Huang's allies, including Trump, reportedly lobbied for his return as CEO. The political context around Nvidia's China policy has been unsettled for more than a year. The Trump administration reversed a policy to allow Nvidia to sell its most advanced AI chips, the H200 GPUs, to China, drawing bipartisan concern in Congress over national security [4]. Earlier, the administration had reversed a ban and allowed Nvidia to sell AI chips to China after lobbying and a meeting between Huang and Trump [5].Related Stocks Climb; Board Cites Stockholder Interests Shares of neoclouds CoreWeave and Nebius also climbed more than 30% Monday, according to the report. The wider market moved higher as well. The Nasdaq surged 6.51%, closing at 24,089.56 points, and the S&P 500 rose 4.73%, according to Just the News [6]. Nvidia's board said Huang's reinstatement is in the best interests of stockholders, employees, partners and customers. Jones said, ""We have every confidence in Jensen and look forward to working with him as he resumes his leadership of the Company."" 247WallSt reported that Nvidia surged 27% after the board reinstated Huang as CEO, adding about $828 billion in value [7]. The company Huang returns to remains the dominant supplier of AI accelerators. Nvidia holds a 94% market share in GPU sales, and the company reported a 56% year-over-year revenue increase driven by AI and data-intensive applications [8]. Its newest Blackwell platform drew the CEO's own description of demand: Huang said sales of the Blackwell platform are ""off the charts"" and that ""cloud GPUs are sold out"" [9]. Nvidia also agreed on Sept. 2 to acquire Hugging Face Inc., an open platform for AI developers, for $12.9 billion, according to a company statement [10].Conclusion The reinstatement caps a 12-day period in which Nvidia's leadership passed to an interim chief executive and back, with the board citing discussions with Huang and input from senior U.S. government officials as the basis for its decision.Â Whether the underlying allegations involving Alice Huang and the Megaspeed export questions are resolved in the public record remains a separate matter from the board's decision, which the company framed around stockholder interests and national security input.References   Nvidia stock has best day since May 2003, gaining nearly $1T in market cap after Jensen Huang is reinstated as CEO. https://finance.yahoo.com/technology/article/nvidia-stock-has-best-day-since-may-2003-gaining-nearly-1t-in-market-cap-after-jensen-huang-is-reinstated-as-ceo-212735744.html  Nvidia AI Chip Sales to China Face New U.S. Export Restrictions - NaturalNews.com, July 01, 2026. The Trump administration reversed a policy to allow Nvidia to sell its most advanced AI chips, the H200 GPUs, to China, drawing bipartisan concern in Congress over national security [4]. Earlier, the administration had reversed a ban and allowed Nvidia to sell AI chips to China after lobbying and a meeting between Huang and Trump [5].Related Stocks Climb; Board Cites Stockholder Interests Shares of neoclouds CoreWeave and Nebius also climbed more than 30% Monday, according to the report. The wider market moved higher as well. The Nasdaq surged 6.51%, closing at 24,089.56 points, and the S&P 500 rose 4.73%, according to Just the News [6]. Nvidia's board said Huang's reinstatement is in the best interests of stockholders, employees, partners and customers. Jones said, ""We have every confidence in Jensen and look forward to working with him as he resumes his leadership of the Company."" 247WallSt reported that Nvidia surged 27% after the board reinstated Huang as CEO, adding about $828 billion in value [7]. The company Huang returns to remains the dominant supplier of AI accelerators. Nvidia holds a 94% market share in GPU sales, and the company reported a 56% year-over-year revenue increase driven by AI and data-intensive applications [8]. Its newest Blackwell platform drew the CEO's own description of demand: Huang said sales of the Blackwell platform are ""off the charts"" and that ""cloud GPUs are sold out"" [9]. Nvidia also agreed on Sept. 2 to acquire Hugging Face Inc., an open platform for AI developers, for $12.9 billion, according to a company statement [10].Conclusion The reinstatement caps a 12-day period in which Nvidia's leadership passed to an interim chief executive and back, with the board citing discussions with Huang and input from senior U.S. government officials as the basis for its decision.Â Whether the underlying allegations involving Alice Huang and the Megaspeed export questions are resolved in the public record remains a separate matter from the board's decision, which the company framed around stockholder interests and national security input.References   Nvidia stock has best day since May 2003, gaining nearly $1T in market cap after Jensen Huang is reinstated as CEO. https://finance.yahoo.com/technology/article/nvidia-stock-has-best-day-since-may-2003-gaining-nearly-1t-in-market-cap-after-jensen-huang-is-reinstated-as-ceo-212735744.html  Nvidia AI Chip Sales to China Face New U.S. Export Restrictions - NaturalNews.com, July 01, 2026. 94,2026-11-24,"Health Canada confirmed it confiscated and is now testing a number of what it called ""unauthorized injectable peptides"" from the shop, Not Peptides."
95,2026-11-24,"The American Prospect

					Ideas, Politics & Power				


The story has it that when Scott Fitzgerald said, “The rich are different from you and me,” his friend Ernest Hemingway responded, “Yes, they have more money.”
Jensen Huang’s return as Nvidia’s CEO compels us to update Hemingway’s line. Now, it has to read: Yes—they’re traitors.
Since the day Donald Trump returned to the White House, Huang kept urging Trump to allow the sale of Nvidia’s advanced computer chips to China. Despite his successes—Trump did allow some such sales—Huang still was not satisfied. Almost one-quarter of Nvidia’s sales went to the Singapore-based company Megaspeed, which existed only to covertly ship its purchases to China, despite the fact that those particular chips were Nvidia’s most advanced and had been put on the administration’s list of products forbidden to be shipped to China for reasons of national security.
This story first appeared in our free Today on TAP newsletter, a weekday email featuring commentary on the daily news from Prospect reporters.
No paywall, no programmatic ads — just the reporting. Unsubscribe anytime. Submitting your email signs you up for Today on TAP, our weekday afternoon newsletter.
In fairness to Huang, he’s hardly alone in his indifference to American national security. For the past 40 years, America’s manufacturing moguls have been offshoring American industries, at Wall Street’s behest, to lower their labor costs and raise their profit margins. Trump’s directive to Nvidia’s board to bring back Huang has been so gobsmacking in its indifference to national security standards that the fact that it also runs completely counter to Trump’s ostensible desire to onshore American industry—the presumable sine qua non of making America great again—is easy to overlook.
As my colleague David Dayen makes clear in his article today, l’affaire Huang perfectly illustrates the primacy of financialized capital over, well, damn near everything in American life. It’s share values über alles these days, and since Nvidia and its Big Tech clients (and loan recipients) are the only things propping up our stock markets, they must be free to continue their activities, even when those activities run counter to national security. In that sense, the redistribution of American income from labor to capital, from wage income to investment income, has its corollary in the redistribution of political power from the electorate to the oligarchy, from the polis to the hoi polloi.
That’s why the biggest investors in Silicon Valley constituted themselves as a “war room” lobbying Trump and Vice President Vance to direct Nvidia’s board to bring back Huang. According to an account in the Financial Times, “a small group of Huang’s supporters, including Uber founder Travis Kalanick, venture capitalists Gavin Baker, Brad Gerstner and Marc Andreessen, as well as Goldman Sachs executive John Underwood, leapt to action.” They arranged phone calls between Vance and the oligarchs to make the case for Huang’s return. According to one participant who spoke to the FT, “Everyone who was important in the tech world was beating his [Vance’s] door down.” Thoughtfully, the FT noted that the people to whom Vance was speaking were also likely big-time contributors to a Vance presidential campaign.
It wasn’t just the big-time investors in Silicon Valley who were urging the return of Huang, at whose direction Nvidia had provided the funds to our major tech companies that enabled them to go big into AI—absent which those big-time investors would suffer a big-time bath. Not surprisingly, Huang’s return was also celebrated in China. The headline atop the STAR Market Daily, a semi-official outlet of the Chinese government, read, “The King Is Back!” (a headline whose letter and spirit give the lie, not that one is really needed, to the Chinese government and its Communist Party’s claims that the nation and party are in any sense socialist).
So, yes—the rich are different from you and me, safeguarded as they are behind the thickest walls of impunity. It’s time we tore those walls down.

To keep making a difference, we need readers to become monthly supporters. When you make a tax-deductible donation of $5/month, you power the reporting that has a real impact.
Mitch GrummonPublisher
